Section 414 likewise preserves compatible remedies, not recovery enforcing a contradictory bargain.

**Proposition-level authority:** The same eight-Justice Part I directly controls these distinct statutory and tariff qualifications.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The willful-misconduct qualification remains effective where independent or tariff-consistent liability exists; no universal consequential-damages bar follows. The denied and unappealed section 202 amendment is not revived. Customer conversion and disclosure allegations do not establish that any independent claim is procedurally available or supports this verdict.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The tariff-inconsistent recovery is reversed, 7–1 on formal disposition; Stevens would vacate and remand, and all eight agree that the recovery cannot stand. Its dependent punitive-damages remand cannot proceed on that basis. O'Connor did not participate.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **MCI Telecommunications Corp. v. AT&T, June 17, 1994:** Applied to the mandatory tariff structure; its delegation ruling did not decide the damages questions resolved here.
- **Security Services v. Kmart, May 16, 1994:** Distinguished because actual public cancellation, not a private preference, defeated the claimed tariff there.

For AT&T v. Central Office Telephone, Inc., decided June 15, 1998, No. 97-679, on the refusal to direct an independent-tort inquiry, see Federal Courts, Unadvanced alternative grounds and appellate relief.


### Primary and controller fraud, connected gains and prospective prevention

#### Securities and Exchange Commission v. First Jersey Securities, Inc.

**Docket or dockets:** 96-1862.

**Decided:** 1998-09-28.

##### Proven direct deception supports primary fraud liability

**Holding:** A broker-dealer and the officer who knowingly directs its deceptive transactions are responsible for their proved primary securities fraud; office, ownership or assistance alone does not establish that violation. First Jersey's intentional concealment of its dominated markets, repurchases and coordinated splitting and resale plan, together with Brennan's knowing hands-on orchestration, supports the liability found under Securities Act §17(a)(1), Exchange Act §10(b) and Rule 10b-5.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Kennedy). All nine Justices adopt Kennedy's Part II; nine direct rationale votes.

**Material application:** The findings establish deceptive dealing in the six securities, rather than liability inferred from Brennan's title or wealth. The firm's transactions carried an implied representation that the prices reflected a competitive market. Its concealment of its own domination and coordinated repurchase, unbundling and resale plan made that representation misleading to both selling and purchasing customers; its duty was to disclose the facts needed to make those representations truthful. Its contemporaneous repurchase prices supply a supported market benchmark in that setting. Illiquidity within the firm's unilateral control does not establish an independent explanation defeating those findings. No automatic five-percent or ten-percent markup rule is adopted. Central Bank preserves its confined private affirmative-assistance action, but direct participation in the actual deception supplies liability here without enlarging that action or importing its private reliance requirements into SEC enforcement. O'Hagan preserves the need for actual deception, culpability and a connection to securities transactions; this case does not extend its source-deception theory to status alone.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Central Bank's private accessory rule remains separate and is not expanded.
- O'Hagan's primary-deception requirements are preserved without extending misappropriation liability.

**Independent alternative holding:** Section 20(a) permits the SEC, which falls within the Exchange Act's relevant definition of person, to enforce controller responsibility for the established §10(b) and Rule 10b-5 violation on proof of actual control and the deliberate culpable participation found here. Its defense requires both good faith and absence of direct or indirect inducement; Brennan cannot satisfy that defense on the supported findings.

**Proposition-level authority for alternative holding:** Opinion of the Court (Kennedy). All nine Justices adopt Kennedy's Part III; nine direct rationale votes. This is an independently sufficient statutory ground at the stated scope, separate from his primary participation.

**Material application:** Section 3(a)(9)'s definition includes governmental agencies, defeating the contention that the SEC cannot invoke this supported controller liability. Brennan exercised actual control and deliberately participated in the coordinated scheme. A compliance arrangement that deliberately failed to examine the relevant transactions does not establish good faith, and his active direction defeats the separate no-inducement requirement. The Court does not impose liability from passive shareholding alone, and the defense remains available to a controller who proves both good faith and absence of direct or indirect inducement. It need not settle every disputed culpable-participation pleading burden because that participation was proved. This Exchange Act controller ground does not become a rule supplying controller liability for every Securities Act claim.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Central Bank retains its distinct accessory domain and does not displace the express controller provision or its conjunctive defense.
- O'Hagan retains its distinct primary-fraud domain and does not displace the express controller provision or its conjunctive defense.

##### Equity may recover the connected gains once

**Holding:** In the SEC's injunctive enforcement proceeding authorized by Securities Act §20(b), 15 U.S.C. §77t(b), and Exchange Act §21(d)(1), 15 U.S.C. §78u(d)(1), traditional ancillary restitution permits recovery of the reasonably approximated unlawful gains connected to the proved fraud, with credits preventing duplicate recovery. Joint and several responsibility is proper for First Jersey and Brennan's proved collaboration and benefit in this enterprise, not because every owner owes every corporate debt.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Kennedy). All nine Justices adopt Kennedy's Part IV; nine direct rationale votes.

**Material application:** Sections 20(b) and 21(d)(1) authorize the SEC's suit for injunctive enforcement; their words do not expressly grant disgorgement. Porter and Mitchell recognize ancillary restitution within that statutory equity unless Congress displaces it. Mertens construed a different, expressly limited ERISA remedial provision; it does not remove this traditional gain-recovery function. The six-security profit approximation was $27,288,099. Crediting the $5 million class-settlement payment leaves $22,288,099 to be disgorged. Brennan's deliberate collaboration, complete ownership, unrestricted access to the enterprise funds and corresponding enrichment connect him to those gains. The defendants' strongest objection—that equity cannot take unrelated wealth or impose another person's unrelated gain—states a real limit but does not describe these findings. The award removes the proved benefit once; it supplies no recovery of unproved transactions, unrelated profits, wealth as such or every recipient's gross receipts. Deterrence alone supplies neither the amount nor an additional punitive component.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Porter supplies traditional ancillary restitution within statutory equity.
- Mitchell supplies traditional restorative equity absent statutory displacement.
- Mertens is distinguished by its different statutory restriction, without altering its ERISA holding.

##### Retained-use value supports the interest award

**Holding:** Prejudgment interest may approximate the value of retaining unlawful gains, subject to the actual credit, causal connection and equitable fairness of the award. On these findings, the $52,689,894 award using the IRS underpayment measure is sustained, with interest on the credited $5 million ending when that amount was paid.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Kennedy). All nine Justices adopt Kennedy's Part V; nine direct rationale votes.

**Material application:** The $52,689,894 award is the prejudgment interest fixed in the July 17, 1995 District Court judgment, not a recalculation through today's decision. The defendants retained use of the remaining gains during that prejudgment period; interest on the $5 million paid in January 1987 ceased at that payment. A borrowing-cost measure addresses that benefit more directly than the lower rate the Government pays a lender; no identified legal or calculation error requires substitution of the Treasury rate. A large ratio to principal, or the passage of years while the benefit remains held, does not by itself transform retained-use compensation into punishment. The actual cessation of interest on the paid settlement credit prevents charging for money no longer retained. The Court makes neither the IRS rate nor interest during every possible litigation delay universally mandatory. Porter and Mitchell's gain-restoration principle supports this confined use-value component, not an unexplained surcharge.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Porter's restorative-equity principle is applied to the connected benefit of retained funds, without a universal rate.
- Mitchell's restorative-equity principle is applied to the connected benefit of retained funds, without a punitive multiplier.

##### Prevention requires a supported risk of recurrence

**Holding:** Under Securities Act §20(b), 15 U.S.C. §77t(b), and Exchange Act §21(d)(1), 15 U.S.C. §78u(d)(1), a prospective securities injunction requires the proper showing of a supported risk of future violations; past misconduct or a defendant's refusal to confess does not alone supply that basis. The systematic intentional violations, relevant prior sanctions, ongoing market activity, retained personnel and continuing control support the injunction entered here.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Kennedy). All nine Justices adopt Kennedy's Part VI; nine direct rationale votes.

**Material application:** The governing provisions authorize the SEC to seek restraint of a person engaged or about to engage in covered violations and provide for injunctive relief on a proper showing without bond. The actual findings connect the deliberate course of conduct to continuing means and opportunity for recurrence. The injunction therefore prevents supported future securities violations rather than punishing wealth, corporate office or defense of the suit. FEC v. Williams preserves the separate requirement for a genuine preventive basis and does not classify every governmental equitable remedy as a penalty. Neither an industry-wide ban nor relief against unrelated actors is ordered. The Court sustains this decree on its supported predicates without creating an automatic injunction from a completed fraud judgment.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- FEC v. Williams is applied to independent preventive support, preserving its limits on automatic prospective relief.

Related questions in Securities and Exchange Commission v. First Jersey Securities, Inc., 1998-09-28, dockets 96-1862, appear under Federal Courts — Claim preclusion and later distinct securities transactions.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The proved primary fraud and independent §20(a) ground stand. Disgorgement is $22,288,099 after the $5 million settlement credit against $27,288,099 in calculated six-security gains; the joint and several obligation permits one recovery, not duplicate collection. Prejudgment interest of $52,689,894 stands, with interest on the credited $5 million ending at payment. The supported prospective securities injunction remains. The already-entered Special Agent reversal is undisturbed; no new investigation, uncharged liability or additional recovery is authorized.

## Standards and Tests selection: Culpable affirmative assistance to a securities fraud

### Culpable affirmative assistance to a securities fraud

**Governing question:** When the received private §10(b) and Rule 10b-5 action reaches a person assisting another’s fraud.

**Current rule:** A person who knowingly or with severe recklessness substantially assists an underlying securities fraud through affirmative conduct can be privately liable. The defendant must know or severely recklessly disregard both the activity’s fraudulent character and its conduct’s assisting role. Severe recklessness is an extreme departure from ordinary care presenting a specific danger of deception known to the defendant or so obvious that the defendant must have appreciated it. An independent disclosure duty is unnecessary for affirmative assistance. The plaintiff must prove the underlying manipulation or deception, qualifying assistance and culpability, legally sufficient reliance on the underlying fraud, causation and recoverable loss by the ordinary civil preponderance standard. Investors need not have known of or relied on concealed assistance itself; no reliance presumption follows merely from this rule.

**Authority by component:**

- Received action, mental state, underlying-fraud reliance and proof: Central Bank of Denver, N.A. v. First Interstate Bank of Denver, N.A — The received private action includes culpable substantial assistance; Knowledge or severe recklessness in affirmative substantial assistance; Triable issues require further proceedings, not a liability award, 1994-04-19. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Stone, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices adopt the existence of the confined action and its received-remedy rationale. No fractured-decision synthesis is necessary. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg; all five adopt this confined mental-state and affirmative-assistance rule, not a universal recklessness rule for every securities claim. Stevens’s Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone, Blackmun, Souter and Ginsburg; five Justices adopt the record application, preserved proof requirements and limited affirmance and remand.

**Present operation effective:** Central Bank of Denver, N.A. v. First Interstate Bank of Denver, N.A, 1994-04-19.

**Exceptions and limits:** Negligence, valuation concerns alone, professional status, ordinary services without qualifying culpability and assisting effect, and mere duty-free silence are insufficient. Relabeling inaction does not make it affirmative. No primary misrepresentation by the assistant, general investigation duty, liability from default, universal securities scienter rule or comprehensive SEC-enforcement rule follows.

**Open question:** The full rules for duty-based omissions and consciously intended assistance through duty-free inaction remain open. Contribution separately requires established shared liability under Musick, Peeler.

**Holdings navigation:** The received private action includes culpable substantial assistance; Knowledge or severe recklessness in affirmative substantial assistance; Triable issues require further proceedings, not a liability award.

## Standards and Tests selection: Primary securities fraud through source deception

### Primary securities fraud through source deception

**Governing question:** When concealed securities use of entrusted nonpublic information violates §10(b).

**Current rule:** A person acting with scienter who owes a source loyalty and confidentiality, feigns fidelity and secretly trades using entrusted material nonpublic information commits deception connected with the transaction when trading consummates that concealed disloyalty. The source need not be the trading counterparty. Duty, deception and the securities-transaction connection are indispensable; superior information alone creates no market-wide duty. Full disclosure of the trading plan to every person owed the relevant duty removes this theory's deception, but disclosure to one principal may leave another duty unsatisfied and does not legalize conduct under other law.

**Authority by component:**
- Primary source-deception theory: O'Hagan, United States v. O'Hagan — I. Deceptive use of entrusted information in securities trading can be primary §10(b) fraud, Stevens with O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer (six), 1997-06-25.

**Present operation effective:** United States v. O'Hagan, 1997-06-25.

**Exceptions and limits:** This rule supplies neither status-based liability nor a criminal mental-state shortcut from Central Bank's private accessory rule. Every confidential relationship and every trial objection is not resolved; source participation in the transaction is not a Court-adopted extra requirement.

**Holdings navigation:** United States v. O'Hagan (1997-06-25) — I. Deceptive use of entrusted information in securities trading can be primary §10(b) fraud.

## Standards and Tests selection: Genuine pedagogy and religious perspectives in graded assignments

### Genuine pedagogy and religious perspectives in graded assignments

**Governing question:** A student’s challenge to rejection of a religious research topic within a teacher-assessed curricular assignment.

**Current rule:** Teachers may enforce genuine topic-approval, research, source and academic-method requirements reasonably related to legitimate pedagogical concerns; religious conviction creates no exemption. But categorical exclusion of an otherwise permissible religious perspective, or a mistaken blanket legal ban on academic religious subjects, is not legitimate pedagogy merely because so labeled. Schools retain authority to select knowledge, method or perspective taught and to distinguish objective inquiry from testimony or devotional advocacy; no universal curricular viewpoint-neutrality rule follows. Summary judgment cannot rest on a merely possible academic justification where supported evidence permits a material inference of impermissible religious causation. The student must establish protected conduct and a substantial or motivating role; defendants may prove by a preponderance that the same decision would have occurred absent it.

**Authority by component:**

- Legitimate-pedagogy boundary and actual causal proof: I. Curricular authority must rest on genuine, legitimate pedagogy and II. The affirmative record leaves a material causal dispute, 1996-06-25; Souter’s unanimous opinion.

**Present operation effective:** Settle, 1996-06-25.

**Exceptions and limits:** Ordinary genuine factual or evaluative errors may remain within pedagogical discretion; the rule creates no classroom forum, automatic topic approval, compelled grade, independent investigative duty or conclusive liability from an impermissible factor where the same-decision defense is proved.

**Holdings navigation:** Settle v. Dickson County School Board.

## Standards and Tests selection: Public-employee speech and the employer

### Public-employee speech and the employer’s mistaken factual account

**Governing question:** Which employer beliefs about employee speech protect a disciplinary decision, and which do not.

**Current rule:** An employer acting on a genuine, reasonable account of speech that would be disciplinable under the governing public-employee-speech rules is protected even if that factual account is mistaken. Conversely, an unreasonable and incorrect account does not defeat a claim when the employee actually spoke protected speech and that speech caused the adverse action. The employee must show protected speech was a substantial or motivating factor; the employer may establish by a preponderance that it would have taken the same action absent the protected speech. These limited propositions do not impose liability for every investigation defect or adopt the plurality’s complete investigative procedure.

**Authority by component:**

- Seven-Justice genuine-and-reasonable-account safe harbor: Waters v. Churchill — A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor, 1994-05-31. O'Connor's plurality opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg, adopts this sufficient condition; Scalia's concurrence in the judgment, joined by Kennedy and Thomas, expressly accepts it under their broader genuine-ground rule. Seven Justices agree at this precise level; only four adopt the full objective-inquiry framework.
- Six-Justice unreasonable-and-false-account limit: Waters v. Churchill — An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech, 1994-05-31. O'Connor, Stone-Zsela, Souter and Ginsburg accept this proposition under objective factual care; Stevens's dissent, joined by Blackmun, expressly accepts it under their broader actual-speech rule. Six Justices agree on this proposition, but the two dissenters do not join the reasonable-error safe harbor or the judgment vacating the appellate rule.
- Motive and same-decision proof: Waters v. Churchill — The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment, 1994-05-31. O'Connor's four-Justice plurality and Scalia's three-Justice concurrence in the judgment support this common vacatur and limited remand. Blackmun and Stevens would affirm the appellate reversal under their different actual-speech rule; their votes are not counted as support for vacatur.

**Present operation effective:** Waters v. Churchill, 1994-05-31.

**Open question:** Unreasonable belief where the actual speech was unprotected, and a universal investigation checklist, are not resolved. The four-Justice complete reasonableness framework is noncontrolling.

**Holdings navigation:** A genuine, reasonable account of disciplinable speech supplies a limited safe harbor; An unreasonable false account cannot defeat actually protected speech; The motive dispute requires remand without restoring defendants' summary judgment.

## Standards and Tests selection: Prospective restrictions on employees

### Prospective restrictions on employees’ citizen expression

**Governing question:** Justification of a broad advance restriction on employees’ compensated speech as citizens.

**Current rule:** Under Pickering, the Government must supply a justification commensurate with the actual reach and burden of a broad prospective restriction on employees’ future compensated citizen expression, including the burden on listeners. A general concern about misuse of office does not by itself justify prohibiting protected expression unrelated to employment; the required connection must be adequately established. Narrower valid ethics restrictions remain available.

**Authority by component:**

- A broad prospective honoraria ban requires justification for its actual speech burden: A broad prospective honoraria ban requires justification for its actual speech burden, 1995-02-22. Justice Stevens's Part I, joined by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplies six votes for this rule and explanation. Justice O'Connor agrees with invalidity for unrelated expression on her separate, case-specific Pickering ground; her vote is not counted for this prospective-burden formulation.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-02-22; the component authorities identified above.

**Exceptions and limits:** This prospective-burden rule does not constitutionalize every payment or decide materially different senior-employee settings. The existing honoraria ban’s classwide remedy has its own five-Justice statutory-remedial ground; it creates no universal injunction for unrepresented employees or judicially rewritten employment-nexus exception.

**Holdings navigation:** A broad prospective honoraria ban requires justification for its actual speech burden.

## Standards and Tests selection: Independent political-party campaign expression

### Independent political-party campaign expression and candidate connections

**Governing question:** Application of the Senate party-expenditure ceiling and the alternative candidate-contribution theory to a genuinely independent party advertisement.

**Current rule:** Section 441a(d)(3)'s Senate-election expenditure ceiling cannot be applied to a party's own genuinely independent campaign advertisement merely because the speaker is an official political party. Party identity or a continuing electoral relationship also cannot replace §441a(a)(7)(B)(i)'s required cooperation, consultation, concert, request or suggestion by a candidate, authorized political committee or agent for the asserted contribution theory. Actual independence defeats that theory; realistic corruption and circumvention concerns do not alone establish the statutory candidate connection.

**Authority by component:**

- Independent-expression protection: Independent party expression; distinct contribution predicate: The alternative contribution theory, 1996-06-26; O’Connor Part I, joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Breyer (six).

**Present operation effective:** Colorado Republican, 1996-06-26.

**Exceptions and limits:** The general contribution/expenditure distinction survives. Transfers between national, State, district, local and subordinate committees of the same party retain their exemption from §441a(a)(1)–(2); candidate-material republication under §441a(a)(7)(B)(ii) and earmarking or intermediary direction under §441a(a)(8) have different predicates. The rule creates no immunity for candidate cash, genuine conduits, sham independence or coordinated expression.

**Open question:** No majority either validates or invalidates all coordinated party-expression limits or resolves every interaction with §441a(d)(1)’s notwithstanding clause. Neither three-Justice further framework supplies controlling law.

**Holdings navigation:** Colorado Republican Federal Campaign Committee v. Federal Election Commission, 518 U.S. 604 (1996).

## Standards and Tests selection: Anonymous individual political leaflets

### Anonymous individual political leaflets

**Governing question:** Compelled identification on an individual’s independently produced political issue leaflets.

**Current rule:** A content-defined demand that an individual identify herself on ordinary political issue leaflets burdens core political speech, including anonymity, and requires exacting scrutiny: narrow tailoring to an overriding state interest. Viewpoint neutrality or the election setting does not lower scrutiny. General source-evaluation interests do not justify a name-and-residence-or-business-address mandate on truthful independent individual leaflets; fraud prevention requires a narrowly fitted connection rather than categorical identification of such advocacy.

**Authority by component:**

- Compelled identification on these political leaflets requires exacting scrutiny: Compelled identification on these political leaflets requires exacting scrutiny, 1995-04-19. Justice Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer: seven direct rationale votes. Justice Thomas concurs only in the judgment on his distinct anonymous-political-pamphlet ground; Justice Scalia dissents.
- The asserted voter-information interest does not justify this identification demand: The asserted voter-information interest does not justify this identification demand, 1995-04-19. Justice Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer: seven direct rationale votes. Justice Thomas concurs only in the judgment on his distinct anonymous-political-pamphlet ground; Justice Scalia dissents.
- Fraud prevention does not justify this application to truthful independent advocacy: Fraud prevention does not justify this application to truthful independent advocacy, 1995-04-19. Justice Stevens's opinion of the Court, joined in full by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela and Justices O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg, and Breyer: seven direct rationale votes. Justice Thomas concurs only in the judgment on his distinct anonymous-political-pamphlet ground; Justice Scalia dissents.

**Present operation effective:** 1995-04-19; the component authorities identified above.

**Open question:** Candidate finance, committees, coordinated expenditures, large contributions and organizational spending remain distinct. Fraud, impersonation and appropriately tailored disclosure restrictions remain regulable. No universal personal-correspondence or small-item exemption is created.

**Holdings navigation:** Compelled identification on these political leaflets requires exacting scrutiny; The asserted voter-information interest does not justify this identification demand; Fraud prevention does not justify this application to truthful independent advocacy.

## Standing State selection: Current Court

## 1. Current Court
